\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{m}Q_{1}^{l}(K^{(l)})\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}P_{n}^{l}(K^{(l)})\Big)\;.

where again
P_{n}^{l}(K^{l})
and
Q_{1}^{l}(K)
, are tensors that are Polynomials of degree
n
and
1
, respectively, with
Q_{1}(0)=0
and
Q_{1}\neq 0
, of which the coefficients are components in wave coordinates of the metric
m
and of the inverse metric
\textbf{m}^{-1}
, and of which the variables are components in wave coordinates of the covariant tensor
K^{l}
, leaving some indices free, so that at the end the whole product
\prod_{l=1}^{m}Q_{1}^{l}(K^{(l)})\cdot\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}P_{n}^{l}(K^{(l)})\Big)
gives a tensor which we define as
O_{\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{k}}(K^{(1)}\cdot\ldots\cdot K^{(m)})
. To lighten the notation, we shall sometimes drop the indices and just write
O(K^{(1)}\cdot\ldots\cdot K^{(m)})
.

Remark 5.1
.

Note that in this Definition
5.2
, we did not include Lie derivatives of
A
or
h
or
H
neither Lie derivatives of
{\nabla^{(\bf{m})}}A
,
{\nabla^{(\bf{m})}}h
or
{\nabla^{(\bf{m})}}H
. We will however generalize this definition, in a separate definition to include the Lie derivatives (see Definition
9.4
).

Remark 5.2
.

The same definition for
O
as in Definition
5.2
, is considered when we use the notation
\partial A
,
\partial h
or
\partial H
(instead of the Minkowski covariant derivatives), where naturally, the tensors are simply replaced by their partial derivatives in wave coordinates.

Lemma 5.1
.

We have,

\displaystyle H^{\mu\nu}=-h^{\mu\nu}+O^{\mu\nu}(h^{2})\,,

(5.6)

or differently written

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}=m^{\mu\nu}-h^{\mu\nu}+O^{\mu\nu}(h^{2})\,.

Proof.

We compute,

\displaystyle g_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(h_{\mu{\alpha}}+m_{\mu{\alpha}})\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)=(h_{\mu{\alpha}}+m_{\mu{\alpha}})\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\big)+m_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}+m_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle g_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}+m_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})+{I_{\mu}}^{\nu}-{I_{\mu}}^{\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})+h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{I_{\mu}}^{\mu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})+h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})+h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-m^{\nu^{\prime}\nu}h_{\mu\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})+h_{\mu{\alpha}}m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h_{\mu\nu^{\prime}}m^{\nu^{\prime}\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2}).

Hence,

\displaystyle g_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})\,,

and multiplying on both sides, we get

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\mu}g_{\mu{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\mu}I_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}+g^{\lambda\mu}O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})

\displaystyle I^{\lambda}_{\ \;{\alpha}}\big(m^{{\alpha}\nu}-h^{{\alpha}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\nu}+g^{\lambda\mu}O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\nu}+g^{\lambda\mu}O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2})\,,

which gives,

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}+g^{\lambda\mu}O_{\mu}^{\ \;\nu}(h^{2}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\nu}\big(1+O(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}\,,

and therefore,

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}\big)\big(1+O(h^{2})\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}\big)\big(1+O(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}+m^{\lambda\nu}O(h^{2})+h^{\lambda\nu}O(h^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}+O^{\lambda\nu}(h^{2})+m^{\lambda\mu^{\prime}}m^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}O(h^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}+O^{\lambda\nu}(h^{2})+O^{\lambda\nu}(h^{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\lambda\nu}-h^{\lambda\nu}+O^{\lambda\nu}(h^{2})\,.

Thus, using Definition
5.1
, we get

\displaystyle H^{\mu\nu}=g^{\mu\nu}-m^{\mu\nu}=-h^{\mu\nu}+O^{\mu\nu}(h^{2})\,.

6.
The Einstein-Yang-Mills equations in a given system of coordinates

Lemma 6.1
.

The Einstein-Yang-Mills equations read in a given system of coordinates, i.e where
{\alpha}\;,{\beta}\;,\sigma\;,\lambda\;,
run over a given system of coordinates, as follows,

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}\cdot g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}\cdot g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}\cdot g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}\cdot g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>.

Proof.

The definition of the Yang-Mills curvature in (
2.2
) gives that

\displaystyle F_{\mu{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\nabla_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-A(\nabla_{\mu}e_{b})-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+A(\nabla_{b}e_{\mu})+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+A(\nabla_{b}e_{\mu}-\nabla_{\mu}e_{b})+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+A([e_{b},e_{\mu}])+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}]\;.

In a given system of coordinates, we have
[e_{b},e_{\mu}]=0
. Therefore, in a system of coordinates, we have

\displaystyle F_{\mu{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}]\;.

We know from (
2.14
), that the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations are

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\big(<F_{\mu{\beta}},F_{\nu}^{\;\;{\beta}}>-g_{\mu\nu}\frac{1}{2(n-1)}<F_{{\alpha}{\beta}},F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\big(g^{\sigma{\beta}}<F_{\mu{\beta}},{F_{\nu\sigma}}>-\frac{1}{2(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<F_{{\alpha}{\beta}},F_{\lambda\sigma}>\big).

Computing the right hand side, we get

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\big(g^{\sigma{\beta}}<F_{\mu{\beta}},{F_{\nu\sigma}}>-\frac{1}{2(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<F_{{\alpha}{\beta}},F_{\lambda\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}+[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}}+[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}+[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}+[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}+[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}+[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}+[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>.

Hence, we get the stated result.

Lemma 6.2
.

,
The Einstein-Yang-Mills equations in a given system of coordinates, i.e. where
{\alpha}\;,{\beta}\;,\sigma\;,\lambda\;,
run over a given system of coordinates, can be written as

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}\cdot m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}\cdot m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}\cdot m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,,

where here the notation
O
is defined as in Remark
5.2
.

Proof.

In Lemma
6.1
, we compute the terms on the right hand side of the equality, one by one in order,

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>\,.

First term

We have

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>-2h^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+O(h^{2}\cdot(\partial A)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+O((h+h^{2})\cdot(\partial A)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+O(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2})\,.

Second term

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}(m_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu})(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}\big(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2})\big)\big(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}h_{\mu\nu}\big(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2})\big)\big(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})\big)\big]

\displaystyle.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}\,.

We have

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{3}))

\displaystyle.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)\,.

On one hand,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})).<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h^{2})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)\,.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{3})).<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h^{3})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)\,.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h^{2})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big).

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h^{3})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big((h+h^{2}+h^{3})\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)\,.

Finally,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)\,.

Third term

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle-2h^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(h^{2}\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big((h+h^{2})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,.

Fourth term

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}(m_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu})(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}h_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2}))

\displaystyle.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+O\big((h+h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,,

where

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}).\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)+O\big((h^{2})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big((h+h^{2})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{3})).\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}).\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)+O\big((h^{3})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\big((h+h^{2}+h^{3})\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)\,.

Fifth term

\displaystyle 2g^{\sigma{\beta}}<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2})).<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}.<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>-2h^{\sigma{\beta}}.<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h^{2}\cdot A^{4}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}.<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,.

Sixth term

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\sigma{\beta}}g^{{\alpha}\lambda}<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}(m_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu})(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})).<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})).<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}h_{\mu\nu}(m^{\sigma{\beta}}-h^{\sigma{\beta}}+O^{\sigma{\beta}}(h^{2}))(m^{{\alpha}\lambda}-h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+O^{{\alpha}\lambda}(h^{2})).<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}+\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}h^{\sigma{\beta}}h^{{\alpha}\lambda}\big).<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+O\big((h+h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})\cdot A^{4}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big((h+h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})\cdot A^{4}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,.

Final result

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+O(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}).

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}.<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,.

Thus, we get the desired result.

7.
The Einstein-Yang-Mills system in the harmonic and Lorenz gauges as a non-linear hyperbolic system

Lemma 7.1
.

The equation
\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}=0
, implies that in the Lorenz gauge, and in wave coordinates
\mu\;,\nu\;,{\alpha}\;,{\beta}\;,\sigma\in\{0,1,...,n\}
,

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\cdot\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big).\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\,.

Proof.

We know from (
2.12
) that the Yang-Mills fields satisfy

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}+[A_{\alpha},F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}(g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}F_{\mu\nu})+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},F_{\mu\nu}]\,.

Since
\nabla g=0
, we get

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}=g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},F_{\mu\nu}]=0\,.

However,

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-F(\nabla_{\alpha}e_{\mu},e_{\nu})-F(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\alpha}e_{\nu})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-g^{{\beta}\nu}F\big(\nabla_{\alpha}(g^{{\alpha}\mu}e_{\mu}),e_{\nu}\big)-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}F(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\alpha}e_{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-g^{{\beta}\nu}F(\nabla_{\alpha}e^{\alpha}),e_{\nu})-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}F(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\alpha}e_{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{\,\,\,{\alpha}}^{{\alpha}\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\lambda\nu}-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}F(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\alpha}e_{\nu})\,.

Since it is a trace, it does not depend on the system of coordinates used to compute it, in particular one could compute the trace over
\mu,\nu,{\alpha}
indices using wave coordinates. In wave coordinates, we get

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}\,.

(7.1)

Now, since

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\nabla_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\,,

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[\partial_{\alpha}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]+[A_{\mu},\partial_{\alpha}A_{\nu}]\,.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\alpha}F_{\mu\nu}-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},F_{\mu\nu}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}(\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[\partial_{\alpha}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]+[A_{\mu},\partial_{\alpha}A_{\nu}])

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]]

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}\,.

On one hand,

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]+[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\partial_{{\alpha}}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]\big)\,.

Now, in wave coordinates, the derivates commute as it is a system of coordinates, and therefore

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\partial_{{\alpha}}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\nu}(g^{{\alpha}\mu}\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu})+(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})+(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]+[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}+(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]+[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]-\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})\big)\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}+(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]+[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]-\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})\big)

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}+g^{{\beta}\nu}(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+g^{{\beta}\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]]

\displaystyle+g^{{\beta}\nu}[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]-g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}\,.

Computing now
[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]
and
-\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})
. Since the Lorenz gauge does not depend on the system of coordinates, but is a geometric condition on the Yang-Mills potential
A
, we can compute it in wave coordinates, and therefore

\displaystyle[\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle-\partial_{\nu}(\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Finally, in wave coordinates and in the Lorenz gauge,

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{{\beta}\nu}\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}+g^{{\beta}\nu}(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+g^{{\beta}\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]]

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\;.

Multiplying the equation above by
g_{\sigma{\beta}}
, and using the fact that
g_{\sigma{\beta}}g^{\nu{\beta}}=I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}
(where
I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}
is the identity matrix), we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}+I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}(\partial_{\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]]

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\nu}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}(I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}A_{\nu})+(\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}(I_{\sigma}^{\ \;\nu}A_{\nu})-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\sigma}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}\,.

We obtain

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}\mu}\Gamma_{{\alpha}\sigma}^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\nu}\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\big)\,.

However, the Christoffel symbols are

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\nu}_{{\alpha}\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g^{\nu{\beta}}\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\,.

At the end, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big).\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\,.

We have,

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]-g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+g^{{\alpha}\mu}[A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]\,.

Thus, we obtain the stated result.

Lemma 7.2
.

The equation
\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}=0
, implies in the Lorenz gauge, and in wave coordinates
\mu\;,\nu\;,\lambda\;,{\alpha}\;,{\beta}\;,\gamma\;,\sigma\in\{0,1,...,n\}
,

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\mu}\partial_{\lambda}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}(\partial_{\sigma}h_{\gamma\lambda})\cdot\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle-m^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)

\displaystyle+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot A^{2})+O(h\cdot A\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot A^{3})\;.

Proof.

Using from Lemma
5.1
, the fact that

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}=m^{\mu\nu}-h^{\mu\nu}+O^{\mu\nu}(h^{2})\,,

we have by differentiation, that

\displaystyle\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\sigma}m^{{\alpha}\mu}-\partial_{\sigma}h^{{\alpha}\mu}+\partial_{\sigma}\big(O^{{\alpha}\nu}(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\sigma}h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)\,,

and

\displaystyle\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\sigma}h^{{\alpha}\mu}+\partial_{\sigma}\big(O^{{\alpha}\nu}(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\sigma}h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\sigma}\big(m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}h_{\gamma\lambda}\big)+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}\partial_{\sigma}(h_{\gamma\lambda})+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)\,.

Also,

\displaystyle g_{\mu\nu}=m_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}\;,

yields to

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}m_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}

(7.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}\,.

We get then, from Lemma
7.1
, that

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\sigma}g^{{\alpha}\mu})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}\mu}g^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}g_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}g_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle-g^{{\alpha}\mu}\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}(\partial_{\sigma}h_{\gamma\lambda})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)\cdot\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big(m^{{\alpha}\mu}-h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu}(h^{2})\big)\big(m^{{\beta}\nu}-h^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\beta}\nu}(h^{2})\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle-\big(m^{{\alpha}\mu}-h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu}(h^{2})\big)\cdot\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)\,.

We have

\displaystyle\big(m^{{\alpha}\mu}-h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu}(h^{2})\big)\cdot\big(m^{{\beta}\nu}-h^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\beta}\nu}(h^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}-m^{{\alpha}\mu}h^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu{\beta}\nu}(h^{2})

\displaystyle-h^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}+h^{{\alpha}\mu}h^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu{\beta}\nu}(h^{3})

\displaystyle+O^{{\alpha}\mu{\beta}\nu}(h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu{\beta}\nu}(h)\,.

We get

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}(\partial_{\sigma}h_{\gamma\lambda})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+O_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,{\alpha}\nu}(h\cdot\partial h)\cdot\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big(m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu{\beta}\nu}(h)\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle-m^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)

\displaystyle+\big(h^{{\alpha}\mu}+O^{{\alpha}\mu}(h^{2})\big)\cdot\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}(\partial_{\sigma}h_{\gamma\lambda})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}\cdot\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]\big)

\displaystyle-m^{{\alpha}\mu}\cdot\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)

\displaystyle+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot A^{2})+O(h\cdot A\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot A^{3})\,.

Hence, we get the result.

We obtained in Lemma
6.2
, that the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations read

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle+2m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>-\frac{1}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}.<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,.

Now, we would like to write differently the left hand side of the equality.

As shown by Lindblad-Rodnianski in Lemma 3.1 in
[
38
]
(in particular, in equation (3.17)), the Ricci tensor in wave coordinates can be expressed as

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}g_{\mu\nu}+g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\Big(-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\nu}g_{\alpha\beta}\,\,\partial_{\mu}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\mu}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\,\partial_{\nu}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\alpha}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\alpha^{\prime}}g_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\Big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\alpha}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,\Big(\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\mu}g_{{\beta}\nu}\big)+\big(\partial_{\nu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\nu}g_{{\beta}\mu}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\Big(\big(\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{\mu}g_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\mu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{{\beta}\nu}\big)+\big(\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{\nu}g_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\nu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}\alpha}\,\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{{\beta}\mu}\big)\Big)\,.

By defining

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}(\partial_{\mu}g,\partial_{\nu}g):=\frac{1}{4}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}g_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\nu}g_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}g_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{\nu}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,,

(7.3)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(\partial g,\partial g)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\alpha^{\prime}}g_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}-g^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}g^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\alpha}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}g_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\alpha}g_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle+g^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}g^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}g_{{\beta}\nu}\big)+g^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}g^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\nu}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\alpha}g_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\partial_{\nu}g_{{\beta}\mu}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}g^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}g_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}g_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\mu}g_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}g_{{\beta}\nu}\big)+\frac{1}{2}g^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}g^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}g_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\partial_{\nu}g_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\nu}g_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}g_{{\beta}\mu}\big)\,

we get

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}g_{\mu\nu}=\widetilde{P}(\partial_{\mu}g,\partial_{\nu}g)+\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(\partial g,\partial g)-2R_{\mu\nu}\,.

(7.5)

Now, we want to prove the following lemma:

Lemma 7.3
.

Let,

\displaystyle S_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)

(7.6)

where

\displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h):=\frac{1}{4}m^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\,m^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\nu}h_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}m^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}m^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}h_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{\nu}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}\,,

(7.7)

\displaystyle Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}h_{\beta\mu}\,\,m^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}m^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\partial_{\alpha^{\prime}}h_{\beta^{\prime}\nu}-m^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}m^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}-\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}h_{\beta\mu}\,\,\partial_{\alpha}h_{\alpha^{\prime}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle+m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\mu}h_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\mu}h_{{\beta}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle+m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{\nu}h_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\nu}h_{{\beta}\mu}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}h_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\mu}h_{{\beta}\nu}-\partial_{\mu}h_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}h_{{\beta}\nu}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}h_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{\nu}h_{{\beta}\mu}-\partial_{\nu}h_{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}\,\partial_{{\beta}^{\prime}}h_{{\beta}\mu}\big)\,,

and

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h):=O(h\cdot(\partial h)^{2})\,,

(7.9)

i.e.
G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)
is a quadratic form in
\partial h
with
coefficients smoothly dependent on
h
and vanishing when
h

vanishes:
G_{\mu\nu}(0)(\partial h,\partial h)=0
. Then, we have in wave coordinates
\mu\;,\nu\;,\sigma\;,{\alpha}\in\{0,1,...,n\}
,

\displaystyle g^{\sigma{\alpha}}\partial_{\sigma}\partial_{{\alpha}}h_{\mu\nu}-S_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)=-2R_{\mu\nu}\,.

(7.10)

Proof.

In view of the fact that

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{\mu\nu}-h^{\mu\nu}+O^{\mu\nu}(h^{2})\,,

\displaystyle g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}\,,

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}h\,,

and

\displaystyle g^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}g^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}-h^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}+O^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}(h^{2})\big)\cdot\big(m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}-h^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}+O^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}(h^{2})\big)

(7.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}-m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}h^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}+O^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}(h^{2})

\displaystyle-h^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}+h^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}h^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}+O^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}(h^{3})

\displaystyle+O^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}(h^{2}+h^{3}+h^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}}m^{{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}+O^{{\alpha}{\alpha}^{\prime}{\beta}{\beta}^{\prime}}(h)\,.

We know

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}g_{\mu\nu}=\widetilde{P}(\partial_{\mu}g,\partial_{\nu}g)+\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(\partial g,\partial g)-2R_{\mu\nu}\,.

We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}(\partial_{\mu}g,\partial_{\nu}g)=P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+O(h\cdot(\partial h)^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(\partial g,\partial g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+O(h\cdot(\partial h)^{2})\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+O(h\cdot(\partial h)^{2})-2R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)-2R_{\mu\nu}\,,

where
G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)
is a quadratic form in
\partial h
with
coefficients smoothly dependent on
h
and vanishing when
h

vanishes, i.e.
G_{\mu\nu}(0)(\partial h,\partial h)=0
.

Lemma 7.4
.

The Einstein-Yang-Mills equations in Lorenz gauge and in wave coordinates implies that

\displaystyle g^{\lambda\mu}\partial_{\lambda}\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{{\alpha}\gamma}m^{\mu\lambda}(\partial_{\sigma}h_{\gamma\lambda})\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot\big(\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}m^{{\alpha}\mu}m^{{\beta}\nu}\big(\partial_{\alpha}h_{{\beta}\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}h_{{\beta}{\alpha}}-\partial_{\beta}h_{{\alpha}\sigma}\big)\cdot[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]

\displaystyle-m^{{\alpha}\mu}\big([A_{\mu},\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{\sigma}]+[A_{\alpha},\partial_{\mu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\mu}]+[A_{\alpha},[A_{\mu},A_{\sigma}]]\big)

\displaystyle+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot\partial h\cdot A^{2})+O(h\cdot A\cdot\partial A)+O(h\cdot A^{3})\,,

and

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}h_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)

(7.13)

\displaystyle-4m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>

\displaystyle-4m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot\big(<\partial_{\mu}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{\mu},[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\nu}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\nu}>\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot\big(<\partial_{{\alpha}}A_{{\beta}}-\partial_{{\beta}}A_{{\alpha}},[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>+<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],\partial_{\lambda}A_{\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}A_{\lambda}>\big)

\displaystyle-4m^{\sigma{\beta}}\cdot<[A_{\mu},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\nu},A_{\sigma}]>+\frac{2}{(n-1)}m_{\mu\nu}m^{\sigma{\beta}}m^{{\alpha}\lambda}\cdot<[A_{{\alpha}},A_{{\beta}}],[A_{\lambda},A_{\sigma}]>

\displaystyle+O\big(h\cdot(\partial A)^{2}\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{2}\cdot\partial A\big)+O\big(h\cdot A^{4}\big)\,,

where
P
,
Q
and
G
are defined in (
7.7
), (
7.3
) and (
7.9
).

Proof.

As a result of Lemmas
7.2
,
7.3
, and
6.2
. we have proved the stated lemma.

8.
Construction of the initial data and the gauges conditions constraints

We assume that we are given already two one-tensors
\overline{A}=\overline{A}_{i}dx^{i}
and
E=E_{i}dx^{i}
valued in the Lie algebra
\cal G
, associated to the group
G
, prescribed on a given
n
-dimensional manifold
\Sigma
diffeomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, with a Riemannian metric
\overline{g}
on the initial slice
\Sigma
, and with a symmetric two-tensor
\overline{k}
on
\Sigma
. The given initial data set is then
(\Sigma,\overline{A},\overline{E},\overline{g},\overline{k})
. Let
A_{\Sigma}
and
g_{\Sigma}
be the restrictions of
A
and
g
on
\Sigma
. We want to translate this initial data set to the form of
(\Sigma,A_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma},g_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}g_{\Sigma})
taking into consideration that we already chose to look at our solution in both the Lorenz gauge and in wave coordinates – this will be useful for a hyperbolic formulation of the Cauchy problem.

Remark 8.1
.

We note that to impose that the solution remains in wave coordinates, imposes an additional wave coordinate constraint on the initial data set that is not present in the case of the Einstein vacuum equations. In other words, we will show that any arbitrary initial data satisfying the Einstein-Yang-Mills constraints and that is in the Lorenz gauge, will remain in the Lorenz gauge, but if the initial data is in wave coordinates, it will not remain in wave coordinates unless a wave coordinates constraint is imposed on the initial data set which we will exhibit.

8.1.
The initial data for the Yang-Mills potential

For any solution of the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations, one can perform a gauge transformation on the Yang-Mills potential such that on
\Sigma
, we have
A_{t}=0
at
t=0
. However, this is not necessarily preserved for
t>0
, since we want in fact to satisfy the Lorenz gauge condition as well. In any case, we can always define on the initial slice
\Sigma
, the following

\displaystyle A_{\Sigma}=\begin{cases}(A_{\Sigma})_{t}=0\;,\\
(A_{\Sigma})_{i}=\overline{A}_{i}\quad\text{prescribed arbitrarily for }\quad i\neq t\;,\end{cases}

(8.1)

and we then look forward to choosing
\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma}
such that the Lorenz condition and the Einstein-Yang-Mills constraints are satisfied.

In wave coordinates
x^{\mu}
, we compute

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}=\partial_{t}A^{t}+\partial_{i}A^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(g^{t\mu}A_{\mu})+\partial_{i}(g^{i\mu}A_{\mu})\,.

We are going to construct, in Subsection
8.2
(see
8.4
), the initial data for the metric such that at
t=0
,
g_{ti}=0
for all
i\neq t
. Then, we also have
g^{ti}=0
for all
i\neq t
. Thus, at
t=0
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(g^{tt}A_{t})+\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(g^{tt})A_{t}+g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{t}+\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{t}+\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})

(since
A_{t}=0
at
t=0
).

Hence, the Lorenz gauge reads in wave coordinates

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A^{\mu}=g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{t}+\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})\,,

from which we deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{t}=-\frac{1}{g^{tt}}\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})=N^{2}\partial_{i}(g^{ij}A_{j})\,.

(8.2)

However, on one hand, the initial data
A_{i}
must satisfy the following Yang-Mills constraint equation at
t=0
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{i}F^{it}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{t\mu}\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{i}{F^{i}}_{\mu}=g^{tt}\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{i}{F^{i}}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\nabla^{i}(\nabla_{i}A_{t}-\nabla_{t}A_{i}+[A_{i},A_{t}])+g^{tt}[A^{i},\nabla_{i}A_{t}-\nabla_{t}A_{i}+[A_{i},A_{t}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}(g^{tt}\nabla_{i}A_{t}-g^{tt}\nabla_{t}A_{i}+g^{tt}[A_{i},A_{t}])+g^{tt}[A^{i},\nabla_{i}A_{t}-\nabla_{t}A_{i}+[A_{i},A_{t}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}(g^{tt}\partial_{i}A_{t}-g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{i})+g^{tt}[A^{i},\partial_{i}A_{t}-\partial_{t}A_{i}]

(using the fact that
A_{t}=0
at
t=0
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}(-g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{i})+g^{tt}[A^{i},-\partial_{t}A_{i}]\,.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle E_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{\hat{t}i}=\frac{1}{N}F_{ti}=\frac{1}{N}\big(\nabla_{i}A_{t}-\nabla_{t}A_{i}+[A_{i},A_{t}]\big)=\frac{1}{N}\big(\partial_{i}A_{t}-\partial_{t}A_{i}+[A_{i},A_{t}]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}A_{i}

(using the fact that
A_{t}=0
at
t=0
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\partial_{t}A_{i}\,.

Consequently, the Yang-Mills constraint equation reads

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{i}F^{it}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\nabla^{i}(-E_{i})+g^{tt}[A_{i},-E_{i}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}D^{i}(-E_{i})+g^{tt}[A_{i},-E_{i}]\,,

with
\partial_{t}A_{i}=-NE_{i}
, where
M
is defined in (
3.1
).

Thus, we choose

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma}=\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma})_{t}=N^{2}\partial_{i}(\overline{g}^{ij}\overline{A}_{j})\,,\\
(\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma})_{i}=-N\overline{E}_{i}\quad\text{ where $\overline{E}_{i}$ is prescribed arbitrarily for }i\neq t\;,\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\;\;\quad\text{such that }D^{i}\overline{E}_{i}+[\overline{A}^{i},\overline{E}_{i}]=0\,.\\
\end{cases}

(8.3)

8.2.
The initial data for the metric

Since the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations are invariant under diffeomorphisms, for a given solution
(M,F,{\bf g})
of the Einstein-Yang-Mills system, one can always perform a diffeomorphism such that
\Sigma
corresponds to
t=0
and such that
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
is orthogonal to
\Sigma
at
t=0
, i.e. such that the metric
g_{\Sigma}
has the following form

\displaystyle g_{\Sigma}=\begin{cases}(g_{\Sigma})_{tt}=-N^{2}\,,\\
(g_{\Sigma})_{ij}=\overline{g}_{ij}\quad\text{given by the initial data},\\
(g_{\Sigma})_{tj}=(g_{\Sigma})_{jt}=0\,.\end{cases}

(8.4)

In fact,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}=N\,\hat{t}+X^{i}e_{i}\;,

(8.5)

where
N
is the lapse function and
X^{i}
is the shift vector, and choose that on on the given Cauchy hypersurface
\Sigma
,

\displaystyle X^{i}=0\;.

(8.6)

In other words, we make a diffeomorphism of the solution such that
\hat{t}
, the unitary time-like vector orthogonal to
\Sigma_{t}
, agrees on
\Sigma
with
\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
, i.e. that we have on
\Sigma
,

{\hat{t}}_{\Sigma}=\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\;.

However, we want to perform the diffeomorphism in a way such that the metric
g_{\Sigma}
is not only in the form prescribed above but also in wave coordinates simultaneously: we will show that this is indeed possible provided that on
\Sigma
, we choose
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{g_{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}
adequately in terms of the initial data
\overline{g}
and
\overline{k}
. In fact, we could do so without adding any constraint on the initial data since
(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}g_{\Sigma})_{\mu\nu}
is not part of the initial data set.

In other words, we are going to construct
(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}g_{\Sigma})_{\mu\nu}
on
\Sigma
, using the wave coordinates condition. To start with, for a solution
(M,{\bf g})
, let us compute on
\Sigma
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}{g}_{\mu\nu}=0=\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}{g}_{\mu\nu}-{g}(\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{\mu},e_{\nu})-{g}(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{\nu})\,,

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}{g}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{\nu},e_{\mu})+{g}(e_{\mu},\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{\nu})\,.

In particular, for spatial indices
i,j
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{i},e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\nabla_{\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\frac{1}{N}\nabla_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{i},e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\frac{1}{N}\nabla_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{j})\,.

Since
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
and
e_{i}
are coordinate vector fields, we have

\displaystyle[\frac{\partial}{\partial t},e_{i}]=0=\nabla_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t}}e_{i}-\nabla_{e_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\frac{1}{N}\nabla_{e_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\frac{1}{N}\nabla_{e_{j}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\nabla_{e_{i}}(\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t})-\partial_{e_{i}}(\frac{1}{N})\frac{\partial}{\partial t},e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\nabla_{e_{j}}(\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t})-\partial_{e_{j}}(\frac{1}{N})\frac{\partial}{\partial t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\nabla_{e_{i}}(\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}),e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\nabla_{e_{j}}(\frac{1}{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}))

(since
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
is orthogonal to the hypersurface
\Sigma
).

Since

\displaystyle k(e_{i},e_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(\nabla_{e_{i}}\hat{t},e_{j})\,,

this leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{g}(\nabla_{e_{i}}\hat{t},e_{j})+{g}(e_{i},\nabla_{e_{j}}\hat{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2k_{ij}\,.

Hence, we impose

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{g}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2Nk_{ij}\,.

(8.7)

Lemma 8.1
.

In wave coordinates, we have

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

(8.8)

Proof.

On one hand, the formula for the Christoffel symbols are given by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}g^{\lambda\delta}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{\delta\nu}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\delta\mu}-\partial_{\delta}g_{\mu\nu}\big)\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle g_{\lambda\sigma}\Gamma^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{\lambda\sigma}g^{\lambda\delta}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{\delta\nu}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\delta\mu}-\partial_{\delta}g_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}I_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,\delta}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{\delta\nu}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\delta\mu}-\partial_{\delta}g_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\partial_{\mu}(I_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,\delta}g_{\delta\nu})+\partial_{\nu}(I_{\sigma}^{\,\,\,\delta}g_{\delta\mu})-\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\mu}-\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\big)\,.

On the other hand, the wave coordinate condition, (
4.8
), reads

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

Thus, injecting, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}g_{\lambda\sigma}\Gamma^{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lambda}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\frac{1}{2}\big(\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\sigma\mu}-\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\partial^{\nu}g_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\mu}-\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial^{\nu}g_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\,.

Using Lemma
8.1
, for
\sigma=t
, we obtain that in wave coordinates,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}g_{t\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}g_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu t}\partial_{\mu}g_{tt}+g^{\mu i}\partial_{\mu}g_{ti}-\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{tt}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{tt}+g^{ji}\partial_{j}g_{ti}-\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{tt}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{tt}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}

(where we used the fact that
g_{ti}=0
on
\Sigma
).

Hence,

\displaystyle g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{tt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}\,.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{tt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g^{tt}}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}

(8.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-N^{2}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}\,.

For
\sigma=j
, the wave coordinates condition reads

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}g_{j\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{j}g_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

We get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{\mu t}\partial_{\mu}g_{jt}+g^{\mu i}\partial_{\mu}g_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{j}g_{tt}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ik}\partial_{j}g_{ik}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{tt}\partial_{t}g_{jt}+g^{ki}\partial_{k}g_{ji}-\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{j}g_{tt}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ik}\partial_{j}g_{ik}\,.

Thus,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{jt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g^{tt}}\big(-g^{ki}\partial_{k}g_{ji}+\frac{1}{2}g^{tt}\partial_{j}g_{tt}+\frac{1}{2}g^{ik}\partial_{j}g_{ik}\big)

(8.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-N^{2}\big(-g^{ki}\partial_{k}g_{ji}-\frac{1}{2N^{2}}\partial_{j}(-N^{2})+\frac{1}{2}g^{ik}\partial_{j}g_{ik}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-N^{2}\big(\frac{1}{N}\partial_{j}N-g^{ki}\partial_{k}g_{ji}+\frac{1}{2}g^{ik}\partial_{j}g_{ik}\big)\,.

Finally, in consistency with the wave coordinate condition, we take the initial data

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{g_{\Sigma}}=\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}{g_{\Sigma}})_{ij}=2N\overline{k}_{ij}\,,\\
(\partial_{t}{g_{\Sigma}})_{tt}=-N^{2}g^{ij}\partial_{t}g_{ij}=-2N^{3}\overline{g}^{ij}\overline{k}_{ij}\,,\\
(\partial_{t}{g_{\Sigma}})_{tj}=(\partial_{t}{g_{\Sigma}})_{jt}=-N\partial_{j}N+N^{2}\overline{g}^{ki}\partial_{k}\overline{g}_{ji}-\frac{N^{2}}{2}\overline{g}^{ik}\partial_{j}\overline{g}_{ik}\,.\end{cases}

(8.11)

Furthermore, to ensure that the solution remains in wave coordinates, the initial data must satisfy an additional wave coordinate constraint to ensure that the wave coordinates condition propagates – this will be discussed in the next section.

8.3.
The propagation of the Lorenz gauge condition

We will show that there is indeed a way to solve the Einstein-Yang-Mills system in a manner that guarantees that the Lorenz gauge propagates in time. In other words, given the initial data set
(\Sigma,A_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma},g_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}g_{\Sigma})
, that we have just constructed in Subsections
8.1
and
8.2
, we will show a way to construct a solution to the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations, (
2.15
), that is in the Lorenz gauge for all time.

Lemma 8.2
.

The Yang-Mills equations, (
2.12
), read

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}=\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}+R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}-[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]-2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]+[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]-[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\,.

Proof.

We write the Yang-Mills equations, (
2.12
), in terms of the Yang-Mills potential
A
and we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}+[A_{\alpha},F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}(\nabla^{{\alpha}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}+[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}])+[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\alpha}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}+[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{{\alpha}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}+[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+[A^{{\alpha}},\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]+[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\alpha}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}+[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}+[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]+[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\alpha}}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\,.

Using the fact that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}+R_{\,\,\,\mu{\alpha}}^{{\alpha}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}+R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}\,,

we get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\,.

Lemma 8.3
.

The non-linear wave equation on
A
given in (
7.4
), reads

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}-2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]+[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]-[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\,,

(8.13)

Proof.

Based on the proofs of Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
, we see that we got the non-linear wave equation on
A
in (
7.4
), by using the Lorenz gauge (
4.6
), for the terms
\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}
and
[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]
that appear on the right hand side of the equation in Lemma
8.2
. Thus, we get the result.

Now, we want to show that solving the equation on
A
given in Lemma
8.3
, with the initial data
(A_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma})
constructed as in Subsection
8.1
, would guarantee that the Lorenz gauge is satisfied for all time
t
, provided that the Yang-Mills constraint (
3.12
) is satisfied.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let

\displaystyle\Lambda:=\nabla_{\mu}A^{\mu}\,.

(8.14)

For a solution of the Yang-Mills equations (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}\Lambda=[\nabla_{{\beta}}\Lambda,A^{{\beta}}]+\nabla_{{\beta}}\big(\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\big)\,.

Proof.

We compute the covariant divergence of (
2.12
),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\beta}}\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\beta}}\big(\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{{\beta}}\nabla^{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}+\nabla_{{\beta}}[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+\nabla_{{\beta}}\big(\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\square_{{\bf g}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}}+[\nabla_{{\beta}}\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]+[\nabla_{\alpha}A^{{\alpha}},\nabla_{{\beta}}A^{{\beta}}]

\displaystyle+\nabla_{{\beta}}\big(\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\square_{{\bf g}}\Lambda+[\nabla_{{\beta}}\Lambda,A^{{\beta}}]

\displaystyle+\nabla_{{\beta}}\big(\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\big)\,.

Lemma 8.5
.

If
A
is a solution to (
7.4
), with the initial data
(A_{\Sigma},\partial_{t}A_{\Sigma})
as constructed in Subsection
8.1
and such that it solves the Yang-Mills constraint (
3.12
), then for
\Lambda
defined in (
8.14
), then the Yang-Mills equations (
2.12
) imply

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}\Lambda=[\nabla_{{\beta}}\Lambda,A^{{\beta}}]\;,

(8.16)

and we have
\Lambda_{\Sigma}=0
and
\partial_{t}\Lambda_{\Sigma}=0
, and thus
\Lambda=0
for all time
t
.

Proof.

First, based on Lemmas
8.3
and
8.4
, we get that for a solution to (
7.4
), the Yang-Mills equations read

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}\Lambda=[\nabla_{{\beta}}\Lambda,A^{{\beta}}]\;.

(8.17)

Now, based on Lemma
8.2
, we showed that the Yang-Mills equations read

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{{\beta}}-\nabla^{{\beta}}\Lambda-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,{\beta}}A^{\mu}+[\Lambda,A^{{\beta}}]+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{{\beta}}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{{\beta}}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{{\beta}}]]\,,

which in its turn implies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textbf{D}^{(A)}_{{\alpha}}F^{{\alpha}t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\square_{{\bf g}}A^{t}-\nabla^{t}\Lambda-R_{\mu}^{\,\,\,\,t}A^{\mu}+[\Lambda,A^{t}]+2[A_{{\alpha}},\nabla^{\alpha}A^{t}]-[A_{\alpha},\nabla^{t}A^{{\alpha}}]+[A_{\alpha},[A^{{\alpha}},A^{t}]]\,,

which yields to

\displaystyle\nabla^{t}\Lambda

\displaystyle=
